**Scope:** The entire `mb_package` family of projects and their operating environment

**Role of this document:** Durable roadmap, status register, and decision register

This is the authoritative high-level project record. Weekly chats—named `week YYYY-MM-DD`—perform detailed planning, implementation, testing, and evidence review. Durable conclusions from those chats should be incorporated here periodically. A weekly plan does not change canonical architecture, status, or priorities until its durable conclusion is promoted into this roadmap.

Status terms used here:

- **Completed and validated:** Implemented and supported by recorded test or live-run evidence.
- **Demonstrated / partial:** A proof of concept works, but an identified validation or production-hardening gap remains.
- **Pending:** Accepted work that has not yet been completed.
- **Deferred:** Intentionally postponed until a stated prerequisite or higher-priority milestone is complete.
- **Tabled:** Deliberately removed from active planning; do not revive without a new reason.
- **Superseded / rejected:** Replaced by a later architectural decision; do not silently reintroduce.

### October 14 viewer refresh/performance concern — investigation opened

The user reports the browser title alternates between MasterBot Market State and Updating..., spending most of its time in the latter condition, and asks about capacity as the viewer gains responsibilities. Dash uses this title for pending/running callback work; it is not a CPU utilization measurement. No callback queue buildup or MasterBot bottleneck has yet been measured.

Source inspection of the installed Alerts payload identifies three independent one-second timers: playback, candle health and Alerts. Playback remains enabled during paused live inspection so source discovery can continue while the displayed cutoff stays fixed. Alerts calls the full controller snapshot, which reconstructs candles from all visible validated responses even when the cutoff/evidence has not changed; health is read separately by both health and Alerts callbacks. Alert history is reread and the table returned each tick, although the alert archive is only written when records change. The chart callback rebuilds a full Plotly figure when triggered by its playback-clock/control inputs. Subsequent local browser measurements below confirm roughly one chart callback per second even with Alerts selected and viewing time paused; MasterBot's actual timing remains unmeasured.

A local server-only benchmark used synthetic October 12 SPY evidence: 49 responses, roughly 64–67 regular-session candle entries per response, and the paused 14:50:02.999 ET cutoff. Thirty unprofiled Flask test-client requests per callback averaged approximately 45 ms for chart, 5.2 ms playback, 2.5 ms Alerts and 0.52 ms health; chart was the largest server path tested. Local Python 3.12.14 / Dash 4.4.1 / Plotly 7.1.0 differ from MasterBot Python 3.12.13 / Dash 4.4.1 / Plotly 7.0.0. These timings exclude real HTTP/browser scheduling/rendering and concurrent request contention, and are not a diagnosis or capacity estimate for MasterBot. A local browser timing attempt could not run because its Firefox executable was no longer available; no browser timing result is claimed.

Before broader viewer expansion, measure actual MasterBot callback latency, request rate, payload size, browser render time and queue buildup. Candidate improvements are shared lightweight health/status reads, cached candle projections keyed by evidence and cutoff, change-based UI updates, and drawing visible charts only when their displayed state changes. Preserve independent acquisition and wall-clock health/Alerts behavior while paused. No performance code change or title-hiding change has been applied.

## 1. Project objectives and architecture

### 1.1 Primary objective

Build a reliable, explainable, replayable market-observation and watchlist-management system that can:

1. Construct a stable daily stock universe from completed-session data.
2. Observe that universe independently of ThinkOrSwim (ToS).
3. Promote smaller nested sets for closer attention: **Hot ⊆ Focus ⊆ Uni**.
4. Preserve raw observations, membership revisions, timing, and acquisition history in a daily journal.
5. Drive a dashboard from either live data or historical replay.
6. Feed explainable intraday and overnight-volume signals into watchlist decisions.
7. Optionally publish selected membership outbound to ToS as an unverified
   display adapter; never use ToS readback as market-data evidence.
8. Eventually support reproducible day replay, historical back-testing, algorithm optimization, and an independent algorithmic-trading component.

The immediate project is a market-data and control foundation. Live algorithmic trading is not an immediate objective.

### 1.2 System boundary and component ownership

| Component | Primary ownership | Canonical role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `mb_market_data` | MasterBot | Schwab quote/history acquisition; Nasdaq halt acquisition; Uni/Focus/Hot polling; daily SQLite observation journal; replay; dashboard; future historical Overnight Volume (OV), signals, universe selection, and data-quality work. |
| `mb_watchlist_coordinator` | MasterBot | Producer intents, canonical watchlist revisions, precedence, reconciliation, publication transactions, verification, recovery, and adapter health. |
| `schwab_watchlists` | MasterBot | API-OV evidence verification, deterministic Focus-r1 production, coordinator intents, and retained legacy watchlist prototypes. |
| `mb_tools` | Shared, primarily MasterBot | Shared configuration and CLI utilities, secure Schwab configuration, scanner command/status interfaces, window/config tools, and reusable support functions. |
| `ToS_scanner` | El-Cheapo | Display-only ToS GUI automation and scanner-side lifecycle. Roster replacement is outbound; scheduled/explicit exports and readback verification are disabled in display-only mode. |
| `sync_csv_v2` in `thousand_miles\toolkit\synccsv` | Transport layer | Live CSV/file transport. It remains separate from the future after-market archive utility. |

### 1.3 Data-plane hierarchy

| Set | Purpose | Current direction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Uni** | Broad daily eligible universe | Built deterministically after the completed regular session and polled independently through the API. The first full schema-v2 session used 554 symbols. |
| **Focus** | Smaller working set requiring more frequent attention and possible ToS display | API-OV Focus-r1 production and polling are implemented. The first full schema-v2 session used 40 symbols. |
| **Hot** | Highest-priority, higher-frequency subset | Coordinator request/policy/runtime is Windows-accepted. Explicit-cadence synthetic acquisition is Windows-accepted (30 tests and four-process proof); real-provider frequency/capacity, market-data checkout integration and production activation remain pending. |

Every membership update must preserve **Hot ⊆ Focus ⊆ Uni**. Membership changes must be deterministic, atomic, revisioned, journaled, and replayable. No observer may see a partially applied hierarchy.

The September 11, September 14, and September 15 test collections predate hierarchy enforcement and use independent Uni and Focus sampling channels. Historical diagnostic replay must reconstruct these legacy journals faithfully and label their hierarchy status; strict nesting validation applies only after a journal declares the hierarchy contract.

### 1.4 Control and publication model

- Producers express intent; they do not directly manipulate ToS.
- The coordinator owns canonical membership and publication decisions.
- OV is a baseline-set producer; Nasdaq LUDP/M is an ensure-present/add producer. Manual overrides have higher precedence where defined.
- For authoritative adapters, `accepted` means an operation was accepted for processing; it does not mean the desired state was satisfied.
- Authoritative-adapter satisfaction requires observed membership to equal the desired target.
- ToS is the deliberate exception: display submission is reported as submitted/unverified and never gates canonical state.
- Network transport and GUI automation are separate failure domains.
- Restart recovery should begin from the last confirmed state plus any in-flight transaction, not merely from the last command issued.

#### October 15 membership-request design direction

The user endorsed fast promotion because it may precede eventual trading action, and slower, stabilized ordinary demotion because it is usually housekeeping to reduce unnecessary load. The shared request/privileged-restriction/hysteresis framework discussed October 15 is consistent with that direction. This accepts the design direction, not an implementation, a Hot polling rate, fixed timing constants or a complete override/expiry specification.

Working rules and October 16 implementation status (production settings remain unadopted):

| Concern | Working rule | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ordinary source ownership | A source creates or updates its own request and withdraws that request independently. Withdrawal does not erase other sources' active requests or guarantee demotion. | Source-owned request identity, exact retry and independent cancellation Windows-accepted; external authentication pending |
| Promotion | Admit qualifying, permitted promotions promptly; retain Hot subset Focus subset Uni and atomic journal publication. | Immediate ordinary promotion implemented and tested; production capacity policy pending |
| Ordinary demotion | Stabilize the coordinator's combined decision across sources; a temporary disappearance of demand need not immediately demote. Demotion is normally lower-urgency housekeeping. | Explicit grace plus minimum residence implemented locally; 60s/120s used only as proof settings; production values pending |
| Privileged restrictions | One application-level Super User authority can restrict a symbol's highest permitted group, retaining ordinary requests for reevaluation. One current restriction per symbol avoids competing override stacks. | One configured trusted authority and latest-only Focus/Uni cap implemented locally; stale clears rejected; production authority authentication pending |
| Restriction lifetime | Bound a restriction to the current configured session, optionally ending earlier; allow explicit clear. On expiry/clear, reevaluate still-valid requests rather than blindly restoring prior membership. Preserve original expiry across restart. | Default declared session-end deadline or explicit earlier expiry implemented and replay-tested; fixed deadline survives restart |
| Forced transition timing | Proposed exception: a privileged demotion bypasses ordinary demotion delay, while an active restriction always bounds the allowed membership. Reentry after clear/expiry follows normal admission rules. | Explicit immediate_cap selector implemented and tested; production adoption of this privileged exception remains unresolved |

This discussion reopens the lifetime details deliberately deferred under D-021; it does not introduce separate timed-removal command categories. No live hierarchy, production poller, ordinary request or Hot membership was changed by this discussion. Routine recovery policy was subsequently agreed below; its implementation/validation and branch reconciliation remain prerequisites before combined live quote/candle integration.

The user also suggested a small real-time active-request display with brief descriptions, especially longer-lived Super User restrictions and hysteresis retention/pending transitions. Keep this as low-priority future usability work, not part of the current implementation. A future display should summarize current request state rather than produce a new row for every market observation; observers and membership requests are distinct.

#### October 16 implementation priority — Coordinator requests and Nasdaq-to-Hot

The user explicitly asked where Watchlist Coordinator processing and Nasdaq-triggered Hot promotions fit after the first October 16 agenda omitted that implementation track. Correct the agenda: after the normal production opening checks, begin a controlled request-to-hierarchy increment leading to Nasdaq LUDP/M-triggered Hot membership and acquisition. Candle recovery and viewer usability remain work for the combined candle/observation Overlay; they are not prerequisites for the independent Coordinator-to-Hot path. This is a planned implementation priority, not a claim of completion or authorization to alter the opening production session without validation.

Existing foundations are reusable. The current mb_watchlist_coordinator README describes ProducerIntent, source_event_id, effective_from/expires_at, supersession_key, explicit cancellation, supersession and canonical revisions. Its output is a flat CanonicalWatchlist, so the missing layer is channel-specific request resolution and complete Uni/Focus/Hot projection/publication under D-023. The pinned production watchlist_polling.py at a082f5441126 has only UNI and FOCUS active kinds/cadences; Hot exists in the journal hierarchy but its acquisition path must be implemented and checked. The controlled prior r1-to-r2 proof ended at Hot 0 and does not prove Hot polling.

The first feature slice should exercise one eligible halt event -> a durable source-owned Hot request -> a complete atomic hierarchy revision -> Hot acquisition -> faithful audit/replay, using an experimental journal. Preserve event identity and actual discovery/receipt times, deduplicate repeated feed observations while allowing a later distinct halt for the same symbol, resolve ordinary requests and privileged restrictions, and acknowledge downstream outcomes durably. Test from saved/synthetic halt evidence without waiting for a new live event; a subsequent genuinely new live halt provides final live-path evidence. Do not turn the startup feed baseline into a falsely new event.

October 16 first sub-increment is now implemented for review: a pure promotion-only sampling projection and an experimental adapter to SamplingHierarchyRevision. One active ENSURE_PRESENT request targets Focus or Hot inside a fixed Uni. A Hot request also adds Focus, preserving all baseline members. The bridge snapshots request metadata, retains producer/request/external event identity, reason, decision and request times, session and baseline hash; the store assigns the actual publication time. Nonpromotion, force, supersession and outside-Uni requests are rejected. The synthetic proof deliberately schedules revisions roughly ten minutes ahead and replays them immediately; it proves publication/reconstruction, not live transition latency or acquisition. Eighteen focused tests and all 138 pinned existing Coordinator tests pass locally; actual journal audit and two replays match both snapshots. The corrected runner also passed all 18 tests and its retained proof on MasterBot, with clean prompt return.

The old flat cancellation API records intent identity but does not enforce caller ownership and remains unchanged. The new source-owned sampling API separately receives a trusted actor_id and enforces request/cancellation ownership against that identity. Durable serialized request receipts and deterministic multi-source reduction are now implemented and validated locally and on MasterBot. External caller authentication is still required before a future service invokes this local API. Expiry/last withdrawal changes desired membership and reports demotion eligibility while retaining promotions until policy is configured. The optional policy increment now implements restrictions and actual stabilized demotion, validated locally and on MasterBot. The October 17 isolated runtime now implements serialized publication scheduling, durable plans and actual journal acknowledgements with before/after-commit recovery; MasterBot accepted all 28 tests and the retained proof on October 17. The bounded review runs that authority in a separate child process. External producer transport/authentication and the eventual deployed process topology remain open. Live Nasdaq ingestion and the missing Hot poller remain later parts of the controlled milestone.

The October 16 policy increment is committed as 45a8db628769f5b8a8fb3e5d7fc765f4a26f3d58. One configured Super User may cap a symbol at Focus or Uni without cancelling ordinary demand. Replacement has no stack, stale clears fail, and clear/expiry reevaluates current valid requests. Ordinary demotion occurs only at recorded receipts after both absence grace and minimum residence. Hot resolves first and retains required Focus. Timers and original expiry reconstruct across restart; reentry starts a fresh residence interval. Configuration is bound into the ledger identity. The explicit synthetic proof selects 60-second grace, 120-second residence and immediate_cap; these are review values, not accepted production defaults or measured live timing. The publication adapter remains experimental. MasterBot Windows acceptance is COMPLETE: 43 tests and retained proof PASS with clean prompt return.

Narrow rules still to specify include qualifying halt codes, symbols outside the current Uni, request lifetime after resumption, measured Hot cadence/API budget and ordinary-demotion timing. Keep these explicit/configurable; the discussion of approximately 30 observations per minute did not establish a safe rate. Verify replay, duplicate handling, source cancellation versus remaining demand, restriction expiry/restart and independent Uni/Focus continuity before production activation. No new request-status GUI or actual trading is part of this increment.

#### October 17 running publication and recovery — implemented for review

The service binds its request identity/policy, explicit timing/window settings and starting journal prefix. One local OS lock serializes cycles across the separate plan, journal and acknowledgement commits. Current input checkpoints may coalesce into one complete revision; no-change outcomes retain processing evidence without needless hierarchy writes. Due activations, expiry and demotion deadlines create recorded ticks. A committed-but-unacknowledged revision is verified and acknowledged with its original actual publication timestamp; an interrupted uncommitted plan is abandoned and reconsidered from current inputs/time. Lost time is not backfilled as invented historical membership. Conflicting prefixes/configuration fail closed. Deleting the original outcome ledger does not authorize silent adoption of an already-owned journal.

Publication lead is mandatory and explicit because the journal rejects backdated effective times. Known boundaries at/before a proposed effective time defer publication. New inputs after the final pre-write check receive a later cycle/revision; already-committed future revisions are immutable. Immediate_cap bypasses policy holds but actual journal effect still includes polling/lead/write/outage latency; no zero-latency or scale claim is established. Existing residence/grace remains request-receipt based. External authentication and production defaults remain unselected.

Local suite: 260 tests plus 18 subtests; 28 focused tests and the bounded real-process proof PASS. The actual-clock demonstration uses 0.4s grace/0.8s residence, 0.05s local ledger polling and 0.35s publication lead solely to keep the demonstration short. Logical-clock unit drills retain 60s/120s coverage. Two separate children exit abruptly around journal commit and restart correctly. Five real-process hierarchy revisions, zero market acquisitions, journal audit/two identical replays and archive-with-spaces isolation pass. Commit bdf5601; unique Windows review coordinator_service_20261017_234453_3f8b4f.zip is now accepted on MasterBot: 28 tests in 2.401s, proof PASS, 60 real cycles and clean prompt return; acceptance commit 98fb375. Next: isolated measured Hot acquisition, then live qualifying-halt evidence; this implementation does not fetch Nasdaq or Schwab data.

#### October 17 admission beyond the original Uni — implemented for isolated review

After this design discussion the user explicitly said to proceed. The opt-in AdmissionPolicy increment is now implemented and committed as 9d72f48f8dbe166746ad3a50dae43c71e7318e16; the review selects session Uni retention while leaving production adoption separate. The paragraphs below preserve the requirement and initial design discussion. A complete implementation/validation entry is appended to the operating record. Local 40 focused checks and 373 regression tests plus 61 subtests PASS. MasterBot Windows acceptance COMPLETE: all 40 tests in 2.405s, retained proof PASS and actual prompt return; acceptance commit 0417b345e2725f4e1e184d3b3202bedb846dbfca.

The user reports that recent production sessions captured many interesting symbols but missed Nasdaq-halted symbols outside the daily Uni. They explicitly require a mechanism to add outside-Uni symbols for Nasdaq halt, manual inclusion/promotion and algorithmic requests. Fixed Uni was a boundary of the accepted staged proofs, not a permanent product requirement. Extend the common Coordinator admission/projection path rather than giving any producer direct journal-writing authority. This requirement changes the intended runtime scope; no source, accepted ledger, published opening revision or production process is changed by the discussion.

Proposed design: retain the original daily selection as immutable opening evidence and record session additions through durable source-owned requests. The effective Uni contains the opening symbols plus admitted additions; a request for Focus or Hot also supplies required parent membership in the same complete atomic revision. A Uni-only request should support simple inclusion. Nasdaq, manual and algorithmic producers all use this interface, retaining independent identity, reason, receipt/effective/expiry times and cancellation. Exact validation and source permissions remain explicit design choices. Morning selector price/volume/market-cap criteria should not automatically reject an exceptional addition merely because it missed the opening screen; distinguish instrument validity from daily selection eligibility.

Proposed initial retention: keep an admitted symbol in Uni through the current session, while Focus/Hot demand continues to resolve by existing multi-source ownership, restrictions and demotion stabilization. This would avoid silently losing basic coverage when the triggering request ends; next-session carryover would be a separate explicit choice. Session-long Uni retention, admission removal and exclusion authority are not yet adopted. Existing Focus/Uni restriction caps must still bound higher-group promotions for an admitted symbol; a Uni cap does not mean exclusion from all polling.

The controlled proof should start with a genuinely outside-baseline symbol and cover Uni-only inclusion, Focus/Hot nested additions, multiple independent sources, duplicate/retried admission, one-source withdrawal, remaining demand, restrictions, retention/removal policy, restart and exact historical replay. Bind each acquisition to membership effective at its scheduled slot, including any batch-count increase. Do not invent observations before admission or interrupt in-flight requests. Admission/capacity outcomes must be visible, not silent discard; specific limits and overload behavior require measurement and policy selection. The current Nasdaq adapter and request reducer both reject outside_fixed_uni, so changing only the feed adapter is insufficient.

The user also specifies a desired Hot interval of 2 seconds preferred, up to 4 seconds. One or two Hot intervals from detection of an eligible halt to the first recorded Hot observation is a reasonable engineering target, not an established live bound. Separate feed-detection delay, request/Coordinator publication delay, wait for an eligible slot, provider response time and journal commit. Existing synthetic timing does not establish Schwab capacity; preserve independent Uni/Focus continuity during the later bounded provider trial.

#### October 15 routine candle recovery policy — AGREED, implementation pending

The user explicitly accepted the following recovery behavior after the request-framework discussion. This replaces the three-consecutive-errors stop rule as the intended routine policy; the installed bounded-trial baseline remains unchanged until a reviewed implementation is installed.

| Situation | Agreed routine behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Temporary network/API failures | Retain trustworthy candles, show a quiet visible warning and retry automatically with bounded backoff during the configured session. Repeated temporary failures alone do not trigger the trial's three-error stop. |
| Interrupted updates | Keep the chart usable with last trustworthy data, visibly identify interruption/staleness and keep independent health/watchdog reporting active. |
| Authorization requiring human action | Show an actionable warning and suspend unsuccessful acquisition attempts until authorization is restored; automatic retry is for recoverable transient failures. |
| Evidence corruption or identity mismatch | Stop processing the affected evidence, preserve it and report the problem; do not silently repair, overwrite or treat it as valid recovered input. |
| Successful recovery | Resume updates and retain the recovered incident in Alerts. Restored input flow does not establish complete backfill. |
| Missed intervals | Attempt recovery from available provider history. Preserve actual receipt times; recovered historical candles enter replay only at their recovery receipt. Keep completeness unverified until checked. |

Review of the existing a775cbc-based capture/supervisor identifies concrete implementation gaps: candle_capture.capture still stops after three non-OK outcomes or HTTP 401/403; its immutable run plans and live startup guards describe a 720-second SPY trial; supervisor health uses a fixed interval-plus-timeout freshness expectation and has no explicit retry-backoff or authorization-suspended state; a reader validation error is reported but does not currently command the producer to stop. Current requests already ask for regular-session five-minute history, but this alone is not a completeness guarantee.

The next code increment must classify failure types, add stop-responsive bounded retry/session timing, distinguish authorization suspension from transient backoff, make health aware of intended waiting states, and connect evidence validation failures to affected-stream suspension. Preserve immutable old evidence, last-good display, independent quote polling, actual acquisition timestamps and bounded-trial reproducibility. Retry constants, precise resume mechanics, audit/completeness criteria and native installation remain implementation work, not evidence already validated.

### 1.5 Long-range analytical architecture

The journal and stable universe are intended to feed an explicit **Intraday Signals** layer. That layer will combine current polling with historical features. Day replay must exercise the same decision path as live operation wherever practical. Back-testing and later algorithm optimization must use timestamped universe membership and stable instrument identity to avoid survivorship bias.

Schwab follows the implemented model; Pushover and Massive entries are design reservations until their loaders, tests, and operating procedures exist.

### CP-3 — Deterministic daily universe selector

**Status: Implemented; same-ET-date post-close timing validation completed. Next-session readiness still requires explicit input capture, r0 publication, audit, and replay.**

Build the next trading day's stable Uni set after the current regular session has completed.

Established design decisions:

- Use the just-completed regular-session **close**, not current intraday or extended-hours Last.
- Use the completed day's total volume for the initial `Volume >= 10,000` filter.
- If market capitalization is calculated locally, use the same completed-session close with the selected shares-outstanding source.
- Build after the regular-session close so the next trading day's universe is ready before premarket operation.
- Keep version 1 CLI/config driven and operational.
- Preserve deterministic inputs, normalized symbols, explainable rejection reasons, reproducible output, timestamped run records, and explicit dry-run/submit behavior where submission applies.
- The optional GUI is deferred and must not delay the selector.

The implemented selector now freezes the Nasdaq symbol directory, acquires a
batched Schwab post-close snapshot, calculates market capitalization from the
regular-session close and shares outstanding, and writes an immutable decision
ledger, pollable Watchlist, symbol list, and hashed manifests. A one-command
production workflow and operating runbook preserve the independently runnable
stages. The workflow also creates a strict schema-v2 opening `r0` proposal and
can publish it atomically to an explicitly selected journal root.

The September 18 production-style validation selected 554 symbols for the
September 21 opening roster. The motivating DAIC case was correctly included
from a $3.55 regular close, 4,105,261 shares of volume, 1,210,383 shares
outstanding, and a calculated market capitalization of $4,296,859.65. Opening
`r0` populates Uni from this roster while leaving Focus and Hot empty. The
September 21 opening `r0` and `r1` were published to a dedicated schema-v2
journal. Concurrent Uni and Focus polling completed the full session and the
journal passed audit and deterministic replay.

On September 23, running the workflow premarket for source session September 22
incorrectly returned `PASS` with only 133 symbols. The manifest showed 6,052
`volume_below_min` decisions versus 1,233 in the validated September 18
snapshot, demonstrating that `quote.totalVolume` was no longer reliable as
completed-session evidence after the next session had begun. That 133-symbol
proposal was preserved as failure evidence and never published. The live
session used an explicit, hash-identical 554-symbol carry-forward from the
frozen September 18 evidence; all downstream API-only stages then passed.

After the September 23 close, the workflow ran in its intended window and
selected 539 symbols for September 24, with 1,295 `volume_below_min` decisions.
Its opening `r0`, content SHA-256
`3c311dbf4543f23329cf8a56075e5c47b61aaf82d490b73908f05cf25f38a36e`,
was published to a dedicated September 24 schema-v2 journal and passed audit
and replay. The same-date post-close gate now rejects temporally invalid
snapshots and records its timing policy and volume semantics. It was
live-validated at 16:44 ET on September 24 and selected 545 symbols for the
September 25 opening.

## 5. Medium- and long-term work

### 5.1 Medium-term market-data and signal work

- The initial Hot request/publication/acquisition slice moved to the near-term queue after the October 15 priority clarification. Basic admission of symbols outside the opening Uni is also near-term following the October 17 user requirement (D-044). Broader capacity/eviction/back-pressure tuning remains measurement-driven follow-up. The user's desired Hot interval is 2 seconds preferred, up to 4 seconds; this is a target, not validated provider capacity or an adopted production cadence.
- Build the Intraday Signals layer using current Uni/Focus/Hot observations plus historical features.
- Extend current-day API OV into historical 3/5/10/30-session analytics.
- Maintain a historical OV store and compute 3/5/10/30-day medians and maxima, relative/unusual-volume measures, near-open volume, price versus previous close, and persistence metrics.
- Add acquisition-time plots, per-symbol extraction, data-quality summaries, and operational health reporting where they aid validation.
- Establish daily data distillation and retention rules so full journals remain auditable while longer historical storage remains manageable.
- Support short-period backtesting across multiple locally collected daily journals without adding seek as a backtesting feature.
- Plan long-term backtesting around provider-sourced historical data—such as Schwab or Massive—rather than waiting years to accumulate local collections. Record source, granularity, and fidelity limitations explicitly.
- Preserve stable instrument identity separately from ticker symbols; record symbol changes, corporate actions, delistings, and dated universe snapshots.

### 5.2 Medium-term coordinator and scanner hardening

- Complete restart recovery from last confirmed state plus in-flight transactions.
- Improve independent liveness checks for all El-Cheapo scanner processes; `mb-scan-status` alone has not historically proven that every required process is alive.
- Define source priority, capacity, eviction, and manual-override policy beyond the intentionally simple POC rules.
- Retain the low-priority active-request display idea: concise current requests, long-lived Super User restrictions with expiry, and hysteresis retention/pending transitions. The user explicitly placed this outside the immediate implementation priority.
- Strengthen full-target verification and drift reconciliation.
- Improve GUI-collision prevention and adapter-health reporting.
- Evaluate El-Cheapo runtime consolidation—preferably a supervisor that preserves separate worker fault domains—versus directly merging `scan_command_loop.py` and `scan_main_v2p0dev0.py` only after the coordinator concept and current scanner behavior are stable. Until then, keep them separate and establish a durable logging/audit contract across both components.

### 5.3 Back-testing and trading

- Keep single-day collection replay as an investigative/diagnostic tool with seek; do not treat it as the long-term backtesting interface.
- For short-period studies, evaluate multiple locally collected daily journals through a separate backtesting workflow without seek.
- For long-term studies, fetch historical data from providers such as Schwab or Massive and identify differences from locally collected polling evidence.
- Build historical back-testing on timestamped universe membership and instrument identity where those inputs exist, avoiding survivorship and look-ahead bias.
- Compare full-journal short-period back-testing with back-testing from distilled daily files before choosing the long-term local-storage/query architecture.
- Add algorithm evaluation and optimization against historical data only after the replay and data-quality foundation is trustworthy.
- An independent algorithmic-trading component may eventually consume the same decision outputs, run in simulation/paper mode, undergo back-testing, and later control live trades. This remains conceptual and must be introduced behind explicit risk, brokerage, order-state, recovery, and audit controls.

The October 13 monitor stop request is acknowledged, while its actual monitor/watchdog exit and final poller console summaries remain unreported. The recovered October 12 post-close PowerShell/CIM timeout and watchdog incident remains recorded without a root-cause claim.
- The October 14 morning evidence supersedes that prior evening's pending startup work: fresh complete-Uni API OV and r1 verification passed at 568/40/0; both pollers and the monitor/watchdog started. Health at 09:30:59 ET confirms both channels RUNNING, their first 2 Uni/4 Focus acquisitions committed, and zero skips/missing slots. Full-day coverage remains unverified.


| Area | Dependency or unresolved decision | Why it matters |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Atomic membership | Transaction boundary, revision numbering, reader handoff, rollback/rejection behavior | Required to preserve Hot ⊆ Focus ⊆ Uni and make every acquisition replayable. |
| Uni selector | Canonical symbol-directory source and availability; price/market-cap bounds; shares-outstanding source; handling of missing/stale fields; post-close acquisition window; completed-session volume source and rollover detection | Determines daily membership and reproducibility; September 23 proved that regular-trade date alone cannot validate `quote.totalVolume`. |
| Schwab authorization UX | Positive encrypted-config acceptance feedback; explicit forced browser reauthorization with recoverable token-database rotation | Prevents ambiguous silent waits and removes the current manual token-database rename procedure. |
| Trading calendar | Holiday, early-close, and next-trading-day logic | Required to interpret “completed regular session” correctly. |
| Hot | Poll frequency, size, admission/eviction, promotion/demotion, API budget | Must be evidence-driven and cannot break Focus/Uni schedules. |
| Focus publication | Relationship between API Focus, canonical coordinator membership, and ToS display membership | Prevents ToS from becoming an accidental source of truth again. |
| Journal retention | Duration for full SQLite journals; distillation format; archive and deletion policy | Current full-day volume is substantial; auditability must survive compaction. |
| Data quality | Treatment of invalid symbols, partial batches, stale timestamps, and API errors | A quote response is not automatically a valid observation. |
| Observation Overlay MVP | Completed-candle visibility, timestamp alignment, OHLCV cache/provenance, and causal joining of observations with hierarchy state | Prevents look-ahead and keeps the diagnostic view reproducible without altering live acquisition or the journal. |
| Coordinator POC | Availability of a genuinely new LUDP/M event for the combined live test | Final proof requires a real transition, not replaying a baseline as “new.” |
| Scanner health | Independent proof that required El-Cheapo processes are alive and the GUI is unobstructed | Heartbeat/command acceptance alone cannot prove adapter readiness. |
| Transport/archive | Exact boundary and handoff between `sync_csv_v2` live transport and future after-market archival | Avoids mixing real-time reliability concerns with long-term data management. |

## 7. Deferred or tabled work

### Tabled

- **Multi-symbol dashboard filtering:** tabled. It is not part of the current dashboard critical path. Revisit only if a concrete analysis or operational need changes the cost/benefit.

### Deferred

- **Dashboard print refinement:** deferred until higher-value replay and membership work is complete.
- **Dashboard date selector:** deferred. Initial seek uses the journal/date selected outside the dashboard; a later selector may choose one daily journal but will not create a multi-day seek or backtesting interface.
- **Active-request status window:** low-priority future idea accepted October 15. Show concise active requests, longer-lived Super User restrictions and pending hysteresis transitions; do not build it as part of the current Overlay increment.
- **Universe-selector GUI:** deferred. The first selector remains CLI/config driven.
- **El-Cheapo runtime consolidation and durable logging:** deferred until behavior, coordinator ownership, and recovery semantics are stable. Evaluate a supervisor versus direct merger of `scan_command_loop.py` and `scan_main_v2p0dev0.py`; first address the command loop's console-only logging, session-based log naming, rotation, startup identity, and durable command/result/postcondition audit records.
- **Full production trading/back-testing platform:** deferred behind data quality, replay fidelity, historical coverage, and risk controls.
- **Separate strategy/back-testing repository:** previously postponed; keep market acquisition/history and its foundational replay work together until a real ownership boundary emerges.
- **Complex source-priority and Watchlist-size policy:** deferred beyond the coordinator POC's intentionally simple rules.
- **Observation Overlay live work:** October 12 viewer acceptance remains unresolved; the user resumed investigation and requested Alerts October 13. Eventual actual live quote Overlay integration still requires accepted live validation, recovery/backfill policy agreement, and PR #8 conflict reconciliation. Existing playback/receipt and synthetic supervision validations remain accepted; broader expansion stays deferred.
- **Observation Overlay multi-symbol, multi-session, and multi-day views:** deferred. This does not revive the separately tabled general dashboard multi-symbol filtering feature.
- **Observation Overlay indicators, signals, news/event markers, and holdings:** deferred until the diagnostic MVP and active-polling hierarchy transition are validated.
- **Observation Overlay configurable bar periods and alternate providers, including Massive:** deferred; the MVP uses cached Schwab five-minute OHLCV.
- **Observation Overlay algorithm, optimization, and backtesting integration:** deferred behind replay fidelity, historical-data quality, and the separate backtesting architecture.

### Superseded or rejected directions

- **Using the ToS Watchlist as the primary market-data universe:** superseded. ToS remains a display/execution adapter; Uni acquisition is API-based and independent.
- **Allowing producers to mutate ToS directly:** rejected by the coordinator architecture. Producers submit intent; the coordinator owns the target and the adapter performs the GUI work.
- **Treating accepted commands as proof of desired state:** rejected. Satisfaction requires observation/reconciliation.
- **Using intraday or extended-hours Last for the next day's deterministic price filter:** rejected. Use the completed regular-session close.
- **Building the selector GUI before the selector works operationally:** rejected as sequencing; the GUI remains optional and deferred.

A previously deferred capability may move forward only when its prerequisite has changed. For example, persistent journaling and replay were once deliberately postponed during early OV probing; they became active work after independent Uni/Focus acquisition was proven and durable evidence was needed. That changed evidence—not a silent reversal—justified the move.

## 8. Operational procedures and validation evidence

### 8.1 Daily operating model

**October 12 closeout has two separately reviewed session gates.**

- **October 12:** Polling wrap-up COMPLETE. Full-day journal audit/replay PASS: Uni 780/780, Focus 1,560/1,560, 483,600 outcomes, zero skips/missing slots/row mismatches, final 540/40/0. Final console summaries match, with zero journal errors, and both pollers finished normally. Latest monitor status at 19:50:13 ET shows both WINDOW_ENDED with zero missing slots. Retain the journal/raw evidence and the separate recovered 18:29 process-inspection/watchdog incident. Today's monitor shutdown has not yet been reported.
- **October 13:** Evening preparation READY: fresh October 12 same-date post-close inputs, all build stages PASS, r0 inserted with Uni 546 / Focus 0 / Hot 0, matching content hash, target journal audit/replay PASS. Exact paths and hashes for that October 13 preparation are recorded in the historical milestone register above.
- Today's journal audit alone does not establish tomorrow's readiness; the separate October 13 build/publication/audit/replay provides that evening-preparation evidence. Morning fresh API OV, Focus r1 and verification, and poller/monitor starts remain pending.


1. After 16:00 ET and before ET midnight, capture the completed-session inputs and construct the next trading day's stable Uni snapshot from frozen symbol-directory and completed-session evidence. Do not recreate a missed `quote.totalVolume` snapshot the following morning; use an explicit, documented fallback or stop.
2. Before the next session, validate configuration, credentials, output paths, symbol counts, revision identity, and scanner readiness where ToS publication is expected.
3. Run Uni and Focus as separate long-running processes. Current established slots are Uni at `:00`/`:30` and Focus at `:05`/`:20`/`:35`/`:50`.
4. Write acquisitions, observations, and membership revisions into the dated SQLite journal.
5. Treat invalid symbols, missing quotes, API errors, overruns, and stale timestamps as explicit evidence categories.
6. Use the dashboard against the selected daily journal. Single-day seek is validated and journal/date selection remains external. Use Stop server for the current candle viewer; Ctrl+C remains deferred.
7. Publish to ToS only as an outbound display adapter. Do not export or read
   membership back; command completion is reported as submitted/unverified.
8. Preserve logs, run metadata, journal, raw `nasdaqlisted.txt` and
   `otherlisted.txt` snapshots, symbol-directory manifests, and relevant CSV
   evidence before any after-market distillation or archival step. These are
   currently local run artifacts; a formal retention/backup policy remains
   pending.

### 8.2 Established scanner command pattern

- MasterBot publishes scanner commands to the El-Cheapo `SCANCTRL` share.
- The command lifecycle separates incoming, accepted, processed, and rejected states.
- Use `mb-scan-status` as one health input, not as sole proof that all required processes and GUI conditions are healthy.
- Run the El-Cheapo command loop in display-only mode; scheduled and explicit
  exports remain suspended and export/add/resume-export commands are rejected.
- Before outbound replacement, make sure the ToS pane is a static Watchlist
  and ensure no manager window is covering the import dialog.
- Stop display-only operation with `mb-scan-command stop --wait 10`. A processed
  stop result with `shutdown=True` terminates `scan_command_loop.py`; use
  `Ctrl+C` only when the command channel is unavailable or the process fails to
  exit.

### 8.4 Evidence standard

A capability should be marked complete only when the record identifies:

- the exact input/session or fixture;
- the command or procedure used;
- expected and actual counts or state;
- relevant timing and error categories;
- test results or live-observation evidence;
- the commit/version when the evidence depends on code state.

A GUI click, an accepted command, or a process exit code alone is insufficient where downstream state can diverge.

## Maintenance protocol

- Weekly planning task: **Weekly mb_package Project Review**.
- Weekly execution chat naming: `week YYYY-MM-DD`.
- Each weekly chat should begin from this roadmap, select only one or two realistic implementation steps, and report evidence and durable decisions separately from exploratory discussion.
- Promote into this roadmap only durable changes: completed validations, changed architecture, accepted decisions, newly identified dependencies, reprioritization, or explicit defer/table/reject decisions.
- Never change a status from pending to completed without evidence.
- Never revive a deferred, tabled, superseded, or rejected direction without recording the new evidence or dependency change that justifies reconsideration.
- Preserve prior decisions in the register; when one changes, mark it superseded and add the replacement rather than silently rewriting history.

The real-store/audit/two-replay focused integration test passed. The runner stopped as designed before its separately retained proof; full Windows acceptance is not claimed.
- Diagnosis: the rollback test's trigger setup used sqlite3.Connection as a context manager, which ends a transaction but does not close the handle. The original pinned store, audit and replay readers already explicitly close their connections. This failure is isolated to the new test fixture and does not identify a production lock failure.
- Correction: wrap the test-owned connection in contextlib.closing, with an explicit closed-handle assertion before the rollback check. No forced cleanup, ignored errors or cleanup retries. A local regression check confirms the original pattern fails the new assertion even on Linux.
- Replacement package: coordinator_promotion_20261016_141902_winclose_e4fa82.zip. All 18 focused tests and the full offline retained proof pass locally. - User authorized proceeding after the source-owned lifecycle acceptance. Added an optional mandatory-settings SamplingPolicy, restriction set/clear receipts and deterministic ordinary demotion timers. No production defaults supplied; unconfigured ledgers retain their accepted behavior.

### 2026-10-19 — Production continuity accepted; cold-start reconstruction explained

- User supplied polling_health STATUS.txt as of 12:34:32 ET, after completion of the full synthetic history/recovery experiment. Journal read OK (0.000s). Uni RUNNING, PID 32580, last committed 12:34:30 ET, 370 acquisitions, 554 members. Focus RUNNING, PID 20660, last committed 12:34:20 ET, 738 acquisitions, 40 members. Both have zero empty skips and zero missing slots after the 90-second grace. PIDs match this morning's pollers and counts match their schedules at the reported cutoff. Accept ordinary production continuity through this check; full-day closeout and future health are not inferred.
- User asked why cold Hot startup took 69.253s. Inspected the unchanged history worker and native initialization/reconstruction code. Cold here means a fresh collector process with no in-memory checkpoint, opening almost a full day's existing Hot history. Before the elapsed tail, that history contains 11,670 Hot acquisitions at 40 symbols each: 466,800 stored Hot observations. It is not a measurement of an empty morning journal, a single quote request, a fixed delay, or a promotion while the collector is already running.
- The worker's cold-start timer covers load_poller/initialize until ready, before waiting for the scheduling clock to be released. Local work opens/checks identities/schema/guards, reads and decodes the durable acquisition-event ledger, checks chain hashes/order/slot membership/timestamps, compares saved normalized observations and acquisition receipts against SQLite, and constructs in-memory pending/finished state and the incremental checkpoint. It does not query Schwab or wait for a market response. The later independent audit/two journal replays are separate from this startup measurement.
- Concrete duplication found in source: IsolatedSamplingPoller.initialize calls self.snapshot(), which fully reconstructs while the incremental collector is not ready; IncrementalSamplingPoller.initialize then establishes guard/file/schema identities, sets ready, and calls self.snapshot() again, causing a second full reconstruction. The aggregate 69.253s timer has no detailed per-stage profile, so do not attribute a precise fraction to disk, JSON, hashing, normalization/SQL comparison or the duplicate pass, and do not promise that eliminating one pass halves runtime. Any consolidation must preserve the integrity and concurrent-append checks; no code was changed in response to this explanation request.
- Normal initialized polling uses the verified in-memory checkpoint and processes newly appended records. A membership promotion does not itself restart the collector or invoke this full startup. MasterBot's full-history final-minute synthetic Hot cycle mean/max remain 301.859/325.023ms. A process restart later in the session can still incur substantial reconstruction time and consequently needs a restart/gap budget; the logical-clock drills do not establish gap-free live recovery.
- Optimization candidates, not adopted implementation decisions: profile/consolidate redundant startup reconstruction first; subsequently evaluate a durable verified checkpoint bound to journal/ledger identity and committed-prefix integrity, so restart can validate a suffix without trusting an unverified saved cursor. Existing integrity checks must remain. Cold-start cost remains separate from the already accepted steady-state history-growth result and the next bounded live Nasdaq producer integration milestone. No source install, production change, extra market request or GitHub publication of private host evidence occurred.

### 2026-10-21 — Proposed interleaved Uni/Focus/Hot cadence analyzed

- User requests implications, not a production switch. Proposed repeating grids: Uni (0,30), Focus (10,20,40,50), Hot (2,4,6,8,12,14,16,18,22,24,26,28,32,34,36,38,42,44,46,48,52,54,56,58). Existing production Focus remains (5,20,35,50); accepted shadow Hot remains every even second. No poller/scheduler source changed.
- Arithmetic across repeating minute boundaries: Uni unchanged two acquisitions/minute, 30s gaps, 780/full regular session. Focus remains four/minute and 1,560/session, but channel gaps alternate 10/20s (previously 15s). Hot becomes 24/minute and 9,360/session (previously 30/minute and 11,700), with eighteen 2s and six 4s gaps per minute, average 2.5s. Hot calls drop 20%. With two Uni quote batches and one Focus/Hot batch per acquisition, scheduled quote calls become 32/minute versus 38, a 15.789% reduction. Those counts exclude refresh/authentication and unrelated clients.
- Hot is contained in Focus and Uni. Across Uni+Focus, Focus-member scheduled observation opportunities become uniformly 10s (previous maximum gap 15s). Across all three channels, Hot-member scheduled opportunities remain uniformly 2s: each omitted Hot slot is occupied by a higher channel that includes those symbols. This relies on healthy workers, same resolved slot membership and fields, and downstream acceptance of observations across channels. Consumers of only the Hot channel face up to a 4s wait for its next slot after effective admission, plus dispatch/response/commit costs; Focus-only consumers face up to 20s. A Uni/Focus miss is not automatically replaced by Hot. Keep original channel provenance and actual timestamps, not fabricated Hot acquisitions.
- Every even second has one scheduled channel start, but independent processes, Uni's sequential batches, dispatch delay and slow HTTP responses can still overlap in flight. A two-second opportunity grid does not guarantee two-second response completion, new provider data, or nonoverlapping actual HTTP. A shared dispatcher would be a separate design choice if strict serialization becomes required.
- Source inspection: isolated AcquisitionSettings/next_slot already accept explicit unique second lists; journal audit derives expected slots from each run's saved poll_seconds. Production polling defaults and the independent acquisition_health monitor hard-code old Uni/Focus grids, and Overlay request-error tail rendering assumes a 15s Focus interval. A future change must align scheduling, health, presentation and timing-dependent consumers, retaining each historical run's original configuration. An isolated trial should measure actual dispatch/return/commit gaps across channels as well as ordinary per-channel audit coverage. No implementation, live rerun, production membership change or cadence adoption follows from this analysis.
- POM/SDEV cases remain evidence for future screening-limit or approach adjustments. The user has not requested changing either the market-cap threshold or the opening OV cutoff in this review.

### 2026-10-21 evening — Trial comparison clarified; explicit halt reconciliation/restart design added

- User recalled the two afternoon interleaved live trials correctly: first 12:49–13:19 ET FAILED, Uni 60/60, Focus 120/120, Hot 719/720; second 15:14–15:44 ET PASS with 60/120/720. There was also the successful morning MPU Nasdaq shadow trial. The two interleaved trials used real Schwab quotes and simulated membership requests, initial Hot=40, no Nasdaq feed; the baseline-only halt discussion concerns the separate Nasdaq shadow trials.
- Both interleaved trials used identical proposed grids and the unchanged native 750ms dispatch allowance. In the first, Hot 12:59:38 SDK call took 3.818881s and the complete cycle ended 12:59:42.361525. The old selector searched after completion and skipped still-within-allowance 12:59:42, selecting :44. In the second implementation a continuously running worker retains the next planned slot; it samples only inside existing gates and records expired slots as misses without stale API catch-up. Starting/restarting still chooses future slots. Exact slow-boundary behavior was qualified synthetically/Windows; no natural live overrun occurred in the passing live trial. Original FAILED is preserved; Coordinator growth remains unresolved.
- Current source inspection: pinned nasdaq_sampling.py treats first CURRENT snapshot as baseline-only, marks new rows before the original startup knowledge floor predates_startup, and retains HISTORICAL captures as historical_only without requests. Its persisted snapshot/pending-command and commit-before-ACK recovery already deduplicate. Reopening the same producer preserves the original baseline; a later CURRENT snapshot can reveal an eligible event missed during a gap, if retained and its eligibility remains established. This is not a qualified guarantee to recover the day's missing halt/resumption events. Fresh bounded live runs deliberately create isolated state and never perform automatic day-wide reconstruction or restart.
- Official Nasdaq RSS guidance confirms current-halt-day selection, haltdate and resumedate queries, and combined-date query semantics: https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/snippets/tradehaltaccordion.html . Main processing guidance https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/Trader.aspx?id=TradeHaltRSS documents once-per-minute updates and query pacing. Field definitions https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/Trader.aspx?id=TradeHaltCodes distinguish initial halt timestamps from scheduled resumption quote/trade times. Documentation was checked; no new live experiment or recovery endpoint qualification was performed. Current-day RSS is not an unlimited cross-date event history or guarantee of every intermediate update.
- Proposed production design: restore/verify durable journals and requests; reconcile missing eligible events from saved inputs plus dated/current feed evidence using a bounded overlap; deduplicate by symbol+halt timestamp; retain original event time, current receipt time, query provenance and gap/uncertainty; send ordinary source-owned requests through Coordinator gates; publish recovery-time membership and resume future polling with explicit recovering/ready/incomplete health. Preserve other source requests and restrictions. Do not treat historical data as live-observed, infer release from missing fields/disappearance, blindly reissue unknown provider calls, or invent past acquisition coverage.
- Under the existing review request-lifetime-to-close rule, an earlier qualifying same-day halt may still justify Hot membership after resumption, provided eligibility is established; resumption is not automatic withdrawal. Production reconciliation/lifetime/capacity and cross-date carryover policy are design work, not implemented defaults. Mutable reason codes after resumption and incomplete historical evidence must be handled explicitly. Lost/corrupt local state additionally requires verified backup restoration, not a silently empty replacement journal.
- Record this as a concrete prerequisite for production Hot adoption alongside Coordinator scaling and broader operational qualification. Startup/reconnect/network/power/process-loss drills and supervised restart/ownership policy remain pending. This review changes documentation only: no functional code, production policy, process start, provider quote acquisition or repeated accepted test.

### 2026-10-22 — Historical Nasdaq workload study and source-owned request reuse

- The official date-range history search covers 43 trading days from August 24 through October 22, 2026. Grouping 4,645 status/update rows by symbol and initial halt timestamp gives 2,300 distinct halts, including 2,046 LUDP/M halts. Daily LUDP/M mean/median/p95/maximum are 47.6/42/91/107. Qualifying unique symbols average 21.0 daily, maximum 30. Nine valid dated RSS samples match the corresponding history counts. Four challenge-page responses are retained as unavailable data, never zero halts. The initial RSS acquisition pacing was stopped and corrected to Nasdaq's one-minute guideline; final totals use the successful public date-range search. The source study and reproducible daily analysis are preserved in `Nasdaq_halt_history_Aug24_Oct22_2026.zip`, SHA-256 db20756ed00c63db1e9739a251a18187623f017a6663c832f3549c49ecfbaaf3.
- User decision: reduce redundant membership-request work while keeping every captured halt/update version independently of Hot membership. Preserve exchange-reported scheduled quotation/trading resumption fields and the system's receipt time for each version. Scheduled times are not confirmation of actual trading; once-per-minute RSS does not establish every intermediate mutation or advance availability. Future strategies must use information only after receipt, avoiding hindsight from final historical rows.
- The new `reuse_active_symbol_requests=True` setting selects a separate v2 producer identity on fresh experimental ledgers. It retains per-halt event IDs/source receipts/commands, but may bind a later halt to a previously delivered original same-source request covering the full remainder of the session. Atomic, hash-chained request reuse bindings are separately validated against their original request prefix. Lost acknowledgement retries preserve the original decision after withdrawal or expiry. A genuinely new event without covering demand still uses normal admission. Other sources, caps and catalog policy are preserved; there is no constant-time or persistent verified-prefix cache claim.
- Local source/test checkpoint is bd6719f8a8c277c3b3cd60309399b8a92cf8ab65, published as Coordinator 14e405a7884d11e984ab9590430d0570604f0e31. The source-pinned extracted handoff passes 341 affected checks in 7.373917533999702s, with a path containing spaces and changed-source refusal before tests. Tests cover original behavior under the selected mode, retained release-time revisions, owner/lifetime/cancellation/restriction boundaries, concurrent coherent reads/writes, corrupt/rehashed/deleted bindings, lost ACKs and native publication equivalence.

### 2026-10-22 client-night / 2026-10-23 UTC — Paced retrieval and isolated recovery supervision

- New experimental transport retains hash-chained starts, permitted HTTP diagnostics, bounded original body chunks and computed outcomes. One scheduler handles CURRENT and dated queries, with completion-based >=60s pacing, conservative monotonic reopen cooldown, <=10 attempts/12-minute window, independent parent/worker <=10s HTTP deadlines and a <=3 consecutive-unavailable stop. No cookies, redirect following, browser challenge handling, provider authentication or hidden retries are added. Synthetic children deny sockets; actual feed URLs were not queried during development. Nasdaq's official RSS setup and query-example documentation were reviewed.
- Nonblocking owned HTTP children let native expiry/publication checks continue during a slow attempt. Reservation fencing and the network OS lease distinguish unfinished/unknown attempts from completed evidence; a lost controller does not leave an unbounded HTTP worker. Source ingestion binds the exact transport completion chain to a durable source receipt. Each explicit new bounded recovery window requires fresh dated evidence, CURRENT baseline before catch-up and a later CURRENT cutover. Old query completion cannot silently satisfy a new outage. Existing verified demand/live commands may continue while new recovered commands wait for that window's declared query scope.
- The isolated recovery authority validates source, request, delivery, publication and native journal identities before advancement; interrupted source/publication ACKs replay without duplicates. Native effective membership, discovery reconciliation and worker health are reported separately as RECOVERING/INCOMPLETE/BLOCKED/scoped READY_WITH_GAPS. Normal rejections, ambiguous original reasons, unknown carryovers and missing feed mutations remain explicit. Prior-day context does not become new demand, and no past quotes or backdated membership are fabricated.
- Registered experimental Hot process supervision uses immutable generation jobs, identity-bound heartbeats and OS role leases. Healthy owned workers are preserved, healthy prior controllers' workers are observed without duplicate starts or PID termination, stale foreign ownership blocks, and only exact owned handles can be stopped/restarted. The durable three-start budget cannot reset when a registry is lost. Jobs specify strict future eligible slots on the existing experimental interleaved grid and prohibit past-slot catch-up. Uni/Focus are unsupported managed roles; two independent live process fixtures remain running through recovery and owned shutdown. The real broker collector is not yet wired to the new job/heartbeat protocol; only a network-denied no-acquisition worker fixture is qualified.
- Final source-pinned extracted qualification PASS: 432 tests in 19.322003510000286s, network denied, production access false, path containing spaces, inventory matching final checkout and changed source refused before tests. Drills cover both deadlines/orphan HTTP, pace/reopen/failure limits, empty/HTML/malformed/truncated/future/conflicting feed rows, metadata filtering, corruption/rehashed transport/source bindings, query cutover, 5/30-minute explicit windows, lost ACKs, expiry while a real HTTP fixture is outstanding, worker health/restart/foreign ownership, missing registries, disk/status failures, stop/session boundaries and native audit/matching replays. Intermediate 430-check checkout is retained as superseded, not final-source acceptance.
